They were safe, for now. But the silence of the hub felt heavy. Kyber looked at the crystalise – their partner, impossibly transformed. At the unconscious boy, a pawn in a monstrous game. At Cryptolock, a wary, dangerous temporary ally. Francky was out there, wounded but driven. And deep within the crystalise, something utterly unknown pulsed with nascent potential. The immediate fight was over, but the true strangeness, Kyber suspected, was only just beginning.

## Act 2: Waking Dreams


## Chapter 16: Echoes in the Deep

The disused subway maintenance hub became their sanctuary, a pocket of echoing silence deep beneath the Zkazena sprawl. Emergency lighting flickered intermittently, casting long shadows that danced with the dust motes. Power was intermittent, drawn from aging municipal grids or scavenged battery arrays Cryptolock seemed to know how to coax life from. Kyber set up a temporary workstation using salvaged panels near the Citymaster, the vehicle itself serving as their primary power source and sensor hub.

First, Kyber focused on the crystalise. They connected portable diagnostic scanners, running every non-invasive analysis protocol they had. The results remained maddeningly opaque. Logi's core signature was the bedrock, unmistakable, but overlaid with fluctuating energy patterns and data structures that didn't match any known technomantic or biological state. It was processing *something* internally at an incredible rate, restructuring, integrating ambient energy. *Like a chrysalis,* Kyber thought, clinging to the hope of Logi’s reformation. They attempted various interface protocols – encrypted tight-beam, direct data-link simulation, even pulsed magnetic resonance keyed to Logi’s last known operational frequency. Nothing. The crystalise remained warm, pulsing softly, its internal light shifting, utterly unresponsive. A fortress of condensed potential, locking Kyber out. Frustration warred with a deep, protective anxiety.

Next, the Boy. Kyber performed a more thorough medical scan. His vitals remained stable, the deep unconsciousness unwavering. The implants were intricate, networked directly into his brain stem and autonomic systems, clearly designed for regulation and perhaps external control or interface. Wuxing tech, but heavily modified, almost artisanal in its integration – Francky’s work, or commissioned by him? No serial numbers, no identifiers. His simple clothes were clean but unmarked. His pockets yielded only lint and a smooth, grey pebble worn featureless by countless turns in a hand. A blank slate, physically at least. Waking him forcefully felt too risky, given the neural strain and unknown cybernetics. They would have to wait.

Cryptolock watched Kyber’s meticulous work from a distance, leaning against a rusted support pillar, cleaning his repurposed pipe wrench with a dirty rag. He’d found some packaged protein bars and bottled water in one of the Citymaster's emergency kits, sharing them without comment.

"Still just a rock?" he asked eventually, nodding towards the crystalise.

"It's processing," Kyber replied shortly, disconnecting the scanners. "Undergoing internal reconfiguration. Logi is preserving themself."

Kyber closed their eyes, the 'Observer' retreating to let the 'Negotiator' manage the exhaustion. Right before the church, Kyber had told Logi the truth about Kestrel. The deep, dark secret about the betrayal that had forced Kyber into hiding: Kestrel had sold out their previous team to Aztechnology to save her own skin, leaving Kyber to be captured and experimented on. The severe cognitive trauma and subsequent escape had fractured their psyche, leading directly to the onset of CFD. The 'Observer', the 'Enforcer', and the 'Negotiator' were born in that Aztechnology black site. It was the foundation of their trust. And now, it felt like that trust, and Logi, were locked away.

Cryptolock grunted, unconvinced. "Right. And the kid?"

"Stable. Deeply unconscious. Heavily implanted."

"So, we're hiding in a hole with a magic rock, a cyber-zombie kid, waiting for a psycho Gardener to show up?"

"We're securing our position, assessing our assets, and planning our next move," Kyber corrected coolly. "We need intel on Francky. Black clinics, Undercity access points he might use, any known Wuxing contacts or safehouses."

Cryptolock pushed off the pillar. "Could ask around. Got a few ears left in the lower levels who owe me. Risky, though. Francky might have his own watchers out." He mentioned a couple of specific data brokers and info-vendors known for discretion, operating out of the deeper Sprawl sectors. "Gotta be careful how you ask. Can't mention specifics, just a 'businessman matching description, maybe hurt, seeking services'."

Kyber considered. Contacting Jensen was still too risky – Wuxing might be monitoring CFD channels after the St. Jude incident. Using Cryptolock’s contacts seemed the lesser evil for now. "Alright. Formulate the queries. Use burner comms, maximum encryption."

While Cryptolock retreated to a corner to interface with a battered, heavily shielded datapad, Kyber continued organizing their temporary base. They needed to secure the secondary access tunnels Cryptolock had pointed out, set up proximity sensors scavenged from the Citymaster. As Kyber moved equipment, they passed the bench where the Boy lay, carrying the diagnostic scanner.

As the scanner came within a meter of the Boy, the Logi-crystalise back on the workbench suddenly pulsed brighter. A distinct spike of energy registered on the scanner Kyber still held. Surprised, Kyber paused, then deliberately moved the scanner – and thus, closer to the Boy themselves, with the crystalise still in their vest – nearer to the unconscious child. Again, the crystalise pulsed, a distinct, warm wave of energy washing outwards, strong enough for Kyber to feel against their skin even through the vest. It wasn’t hostile, more like… resonance. Recognition?

Kyber frowned, moving back. The crystalise subsided to its normal, gentle rhythm. What was that? Was Logi reacting to the Boy’s implants? Or was it something deeper, related to the Resonator, the entity, the reason Francky kept the Boy close? Another mystery stacked onto the pile.

Later, after setting rudimentary trip sensors near the main access points, Kyber established a watch schedule. Cryptolock took the first shift, his imposing silhouette barely visible in the gloom near the blast doors. Kyber sat near the workstation, the Logi-crystalise resting beside their hand. They reached out, tracing the smooth, warm facets. The faint, rhythmic pulse felt like a fragile heartbeat in the oppressive silence of the deep hub. Waiting. Watching. Wondering what new form their partner would take, and what dangers stirred in the wounded city above.

## Chapter 17: Stirrings in the Dark

Hours bled into each other in the subterranean gloom, marked only by the intermittent flicker of the emergency lights and the low hum of the Citymaster’s standby systems. Kyber took the second watch, the silence pressing in, broken only by the distant drip of water and the soft, rhythmic pulse of the Logi-crystalise resting nearby. Kyber used the time to run passive environmental scans, mapping the immediate tunnel network, searching for unexpected energy signatures, while part of their mind constantly looped back to the crystalise, trying to parse the complex, inaccessible processes running within what they believed was their partner’s transforming core.

Cryptolock eventually emerged from the shadows near the makeshift comm station he’d set up, scrubbing a hand over his face. He looked tired, the adrenaline from the fight replaced by weary vigilance.

“Anything?” Kyber asked quietly.

“Maybe.” Cryptolock slumped onto a rusted metal crate. “Put feelers out through three cut-outs. Two came back blank – either no chatter or my contacts are spooked. Third one… heard whispers. Someone matching Francky’s general description – flashy suit, looked like he’d tangled with a gravel truck – was asking after a high-end ripperdoc, specializes in black market cyber and bio-augments. Near the Undercity entry point past the old Tar Creek pumping station.”

“Could be him,” Kyber mused. The location made sense if Francky had Undercity connections or needed serious, off-the-books medical help. “But it’s thin.”

“Thinnest there is,” Cryptolock agreed. “Could be any slickster who picked a fight he couldn’t win. Spreading that description around wider is risky. Francky finds out we’re actively hunting him, he might bolt, or worse, set a trap.”

“Agreed. We hold for now. Strengthen our position here, learn more about…” Kyber glanced towards the unconscious Boy, then at the crystalise. “…our current assets.”

Cryptolock followed their gaze, skepticism etched on his face. “Still think that rock’s gonna wake up and make breakfast?”

“Logi is restructuring,” Kyber stated firmly, more for their own benefit than Cryptolock’s. “The process is complex. Interrupting it…” They let the sentence hang. An idea formed, driven by the earlier anomalous reaction. “But observation might yield data.”

Before Cryptolock could argue, Kyber carefully picked up the Logi-crystalise. Its faint warmth felt constant, reassuring. They carried it over to the bench where the Boy lay motionless. Slowly, deliberately, Kyber placed the crystalise on the bench beside the Boy’s head, activating their scanner to monitor both.

Immediately, the crystalise reacted. The soft pulse quickened, the internal light brightening, shifting from a cool white to warmer, golden tones. The energy readings Kyber’s scanner picked up spiked, not dangerously, but significantly, focused in a tight field encompassing the crystalise and the Boy’s head. It felt like… concentrated attention.

“What’s it doing?” Cryptolock asked, moving closer, his earlier sarcasm replaced by wary curiosity.

“I think,” Kyber said slowly, watching the readouts, interpreting through the lens of their assumption, “Logi is scanning him. Assessing the implants, the neural state. Trying to understand his condition, maybe find a way to help him.” *Or assess the threat he represents,* a colder part of Kyber added.

They watched in silence for several minutes. The crystalise maintained its heightened state, pulsing steadily, bathing the Boy’s face in soft, shifting light. Then, the Boy stirred.

A low groan escaped his lips, barely audible. His eyelids fluttered, translucent membranes flickering across unresponsive pupils. His fingers twitched, clenching weakly against the rough fabric of the bench.

As he moved, the Logi-crystalise pulsed sharply, emitting a wave of warmth Kyber felt even standing a meter away. The internal light flared, the complex patterns swirling rapidly.

The Boy’s groans grew louder, tinged with fear or pain. His head turned restlessly. Kyber and Cryptolock instinctively tensed, ready for anything – a seizure, a violent awakening, some kind of psychic backlash.

Slowly, sluggishly, the Boy’s eyes cracked open. They were dark, unfocused, darting frantically around the dim, unfamiliar surroundings before finally settling, with dawning awareness and a flicker of profound terror, on the pulsing, glowing crystalise resting inches from his face.

## Chapter 18: Spark

Terror flared in the Boy’s newly opened eyes. They locked onto the pulsing Logi-crystalise beside him, pupils dilating wildly. A choked gasp escaped his lips, and he scrambled backwards on the bench, trying desperately to put distance between himself and the softly glowing object, tangling himself in the thin blanket Kyber had draped over him. His breathing hitched, accelerating into shallow hyperventilation.

“Easy,” Kyber said, raising their hands slowly, palms out, activating the Negotiator persona’s most calming vocal modulations. “You’re safe here. No one’s going to hurt you.”

Cryptolock shifted beside Kyber, his hand instinctively hovering near the pipe wrench strapped to his thigh. His gaze was hard, suspicious, watching the Boy’s panic for any sign of deception or programmed aggression.

The crystalise reacted instantly to the Boy's distress. The warm golden light softened, the rapid pulsing slowing to the gentle, rhythmic beat Kyber had first observed. The energy field contracted slightly, the feeling of intense *attention* lessening, as if deliberately trying to appear less threatening. *Logi’s trying to calm him,* Kyber thought, relieved and concerned. *They sense his fear.*

“Where…?” the Boy stammered, his voice thin and reedy, eyes darting frantically around the dim, unfamiliar subway hub before snapping back to the crystalise, unable to look away for long. “The light… it burns… Make it stop\!” He flinched, squeezing his eyes shut, even though the crystalise’s light was now soft.

“The light won’t hurt you,” Kyber said gently, kneeling down to be closer to his eye level, keeping a careful distance. “That light… it’s a friend. It helped stop the bad man.” A necessary simplification, Kyber reasoned.

The Boy cracked his eyes open again, confusion warring with the ingrained fear. “Friend?” He looked from Kyber’s reassuring expression to Cryptolock’s grim visage, flinching slightly from the ghoul’s appearance, then inevitably back to the softly glowing crystal.

“Yeah, kid,” Cryptolock’s voice was rough, but he made an effort to keep it low. “The Gardener. Francky. Where is he?”

The name ‘Gardener’ made the Boy recoil again, pressing himself against the cold concrete wall behind the bench. “Gardener… makes the seeds grow…” he whispered, his voice trembling. Tears welled in his eyes. “Hurts… The box hurts…” He wrapped his arms around himself, rocking slightly. Memories, fragmented and painful, were clearly surfacing.

Kyber held up a hand to forestall Cryptolock’s next question. Pushing too hard, too fast, would shut him down. “It’s okay,” Kyber repeated softly. “The box is gone. Francky is gone. You’re away from them now.” They paused, letting the words sink in. “What’s your name? Can you tell us your name?”

The Boy shook his head mutely, still trembling, his gaze fixed on the crystalise again. The terror seemed to be receding slightly, replaced by a kind of exhausted, bewildered fixation. He stared at the shifting patterns of light within the crystal’s depths as if seeing something only he could perceive.

“He… Gardener called me Spark,” the Boy whispered, so quietly Kyber almost missed it. “Said… said I had to hold the Spark. Keep it safe… until the Potential was ready.” He reached out a trembling finger, not quite touching the crystalise, tracing its shape in the air. “The heart… is it still beating?”

*Spark? Potential? The heart?* Kyber mentally filed away the terms. Francky’s twisted metaphors, now given context by the Boy’s fragmented memories. He wasn’t just a vessel; he was meant to be a *guardian* for whatever was inside the Resonator, the "wisp of life" Logi had inadvertently saved and which now resided, presumably, within the crystalise.

The crystalise pulsed gently, seemingly in response to the Boy’s proximity and attention, the light swirling within. The Boy watched, transfixed, his breathing slowly evening out, exhaustion pulling at him even as his gaze remained locked on the softly glowing object that both terrified and fascinated him. He hadn’t answered their questions directly, but he’d given them crucial pieces of Francky’s perverse puzzle. And he’d given them a name – Spark.

## Chapter 19: Gardener, Seed, and Spark

"Spark?" Kyber repeated softly, holding onto the name. "Francky called you Spark?" The Boy nodded weakly, his eyes still fixed on the Logi-crystalise, though the frantic terror had subsided into a kind of weary awe. "And the heart you mentioned... the one in the box? Is it... in there?" Kyber gestured carefully towards the crystalise.

Spark frowned, a flicker of confusion crossing his pale features. "The Gardener... he said the Resonator held the heart, the first spark. I just... watched it. Kept it safe." His gaze drifted around the dim hub. "It's loud here. Quieter than the church, but... loud." He seemed to be picking up on the ambient tech hum, the Citymaster's systems, perhaps even Kyber's own cybernetics, with unnerving sensitivity.

"Implants," Cryptolock growled quietly from beside Kyber. "Kid's wired for something." He addressed Spark directly, his voice surprisingly less harsh than before. "Kid – Spark – did the Gardener hurt you? When the box...?"

Spark flinched at the memory. "Always hurts when he works. The box… sang wrong. Too loud." He shivered. "Gardener got mad."

So, Francky was definitely the Gardener. The Resonator box held the "heart" or "spark" – the wisp of life Logi saved? – and Spark's role was guardian or monitor. The implants likely facilitated this connection. And Logi's intervention had caused a backlash that hurt Spark, reinforcing the idea of a link. Kyber processed the fragments, the picture becoming slightly clearer, and infinitely stranger. Francky wasn't just making copies; he was trying to cultivate or transfer something vital, using the Boy as part of the apparatus. Protecting Spark, and the crystalise which now seemingly held that "heart," became even more critical.

"Okay, Spark," Kyber said, retrieving a water pouch and a nutrient paste tube from the medkit. "You need fluids, energy. Can you drink?"

Spark accepted the water numbly, sipping weakly. He waved away the nutrient paste. Kyber checked his vitals again – weak, dehydrated, but stable. No obvious new injuries from the blast or his collapse. Exhaustion seemed the primary issue now. Kyber helped make him slightly more comfortable on the bench, propping his head with a bundled jacket.

"Alright," Kyber said, turning back to Cryptolock, keeping their voice low. "Spark confirms Francky is the 'Gardener'. He was using Spark somehow to monitor or guard the 'heart' in the Resonator. Logi's action saved that 'heart', embedding it with their own signature into the crystalise." Kyber still framed it through their own hopeful lens – Logi *protected* the heart, incorporated it, maybe? "And the process hurt Spark. Francky will definitely want him back, and probably the crystalise too."

Cryptolock nodded grimly. "Makes the ripperdoc rumour more likely. If he's badly hurt and needs specialized gear or help related to whatever this Spark/heart shit is, that's where he'd go. But it's still just a rumour." Just then, the burner comm Cryptolock had used earlier chirped softly. He checked the message, his expression tightening.

"Frag," he muttered. "One of my eyes near Sector 7 reports Wuxing Security crawling all over the St. Jude's area. Full sweep teams, sensor drones. They're locking down the surrounding blocks."

Wuxing was moving, faster than Kyber expected. The energy discharge hadn't gone unnoticed. Their window for quiet operation was closing rapidly.

"They'll be running scans, looking for energy signatures, tracking back any Matrix activity," Kyber analyzed quickly. "This hub might be shielded, but for how long? We can't stay here indefinitely."

"So we hit the ripperdoc lead?" Cryptolock asked.

Kyber considered, glancing at the crystalise, then at Spark who seemed to be drifting into an exhausted doze. "Too risky. We go hunting Francky now, we risk leading him or Wuxing back here. And I won't move Logi," they gestured to the crystalise, "or the boy until I understand more. We need more intel, passively acquired if possible. And we need to reinforce this location."

Cryptolock didn't look happy about waiting, but he didn't argue. He understood Zkazena logic: sometimes the best move was to go deep and wait for the heat to pass or the prey to make a mistake. "Fine. I know a few tricks to 'discourage' visitors down here. And I can try pinging another contact, someone deeper underground, see if Francky surfaced anywhere else."

"Good. I'll set up enhanced passive sensors from the Citymaster's kit at the access points," Kyber agreed. "And I'm staying with Logi and the boy."

As Cryptolock moved off to secure the perimeter and try his contacts again, Kyber settled onto a crate near the workbench, pulling a datapad towards them to review Logi’s partial data burst from before the overload. The Logi-crystalise pulsed softly nearby. Spark murmured in his sleep, reaching out unconsciously towards the crystalise before his hand fell back, clutching the smooth grey pebble he must have produced from somewhere. The rhythmic light washed over him, constant, watchful. Waiting.

## Chapter 20: Resonance and Containment

The hours in the subway hub crawled by, measured in the drip of unseen water and the soft, rhythmic glow of the Logi-crystalise. A tense quiet settled over their makeshift sanctuary. Cryptolock, after securing the secondary tunnels with a combination of scavenged tech and crude but effective physical barriers, monitored his encrypted comm channels, occasionally nodding off in a battered chair, wrench always within reach. Spark slept fitfully on the bench, sometimes murmuring unintelligible phrases – "light," "seed," "Gardener" – his small hand occasionally twitching or clutching the smooth grey pebble.

Kyber used the relative calm to meticulously analyze the corrupted data fragment salvaged from Logi's final moments. It was a mess – signal degradation, energy scorching, encryption wrapping around encryption. But fragments resolved under Kyber's patient digital archaeology. There were partial schematics that looked disturbingly like a hybrid of Wuxing biotechnology and the pseudo-occult diagrams they'd seen chalked on the floor of St. Jude's. Snippets of text surfaced: "...Leónization resonant frequency," "...astral harmonic catalyst," "...psycho-spiritual architecture integration," "...stabilizing vessel 'Spark'..."

Leónization. Wuxing's fountain of youth, their most guarded corporate secret. Francky's "gardening" wasn't just some back-alley occultism; it seemed to be parasitically tapping into or hideously repurposing Wuxing's own high-end rejuvenation technology, blending it with something far stranger, something involving astral space and psychic structures. And Spark was explicitly named as the "vessel."

Kyber glanced at the sleeping boy, then at the crystalise. Its gentle pulse seemed steady, reassuring. Kyber ran another passive scan, correlating its energy output with Spark's neural activity. There was a subtle, undeniable synchronization, a resonance between the crystal's pulsing light and the boy's own faint bio-signature, especially the non-standard implants. *Logi's analyzing the connection,* Kyber interpreted, *mapping the interface between Spark and whatever Francky intended.* It felt like progress, however slow.

As Kyber focused intently on the shifting data patterns, the air in the hub seemed to waver for a second. The sharp lines of the console blurred, overlaid again with that fleeting vision of intricate, impossible geometry. This time it lasted longer, accompanied by a faint, discordant chime that echoed only in Kyber’s skull. They blinked hard, shaking their head, the vision receding. Residual effects from the Resonator blast? Or something else? The feeling of being watched, not by physical eyes, but by something ambient, informational, prickled at the edge of their awareness.

A harsh buzz from Cryptolock’s burner comm startled them both. He listened intently, his expression growing grimmer.

"Frag me," he finally muttered, cutting the connection. "Heat's coming down. Hard."

"Wuxing?" Kyber asked, already suspecting the answer.

"Yeah. Full-blown containment teams moving into the St. Jude's perimeter. Not just corpsec goons – these are the science division specialists. Biohazard suits, arcane scanners, heavy energy dampeners. They ain't just cleaning up; they're *hunting* for something specific. My contact said they're running deep scans, looking for residual energy signatures matching the blast, unique bio-traces..." He didn't need to finish the sentence. They had the source of a unique energy signature – the crystalise – and a unique bio-trace – Spark – right here with them.

The fragile safety of the hub evaporated. Wuxing wasn't just investigating; they were deploying serious assets. If they detected the crystalise's energy, or if Francky somehow tipped them off, this deep hole wouldn't stay hidden for long.

Kyber stared at the tactical map displayed on their datapad, the blinking icons representing Wuxing's tightening cordon around the blast site feeling like the jaws of a trap closing in. Waiting passively was no longer an option. They needed to move, or they needed a plan to deal with Wuxing directly.

"They're escalating," Kyber said, voice tight. "Which means they know, or suspect, how badly their tech was compromised or what Francky was really doing." They looked from the vulnerable, sleeping boy to the softly pulsing crystalise that held, Kyber fiercely believed, their partner's fragile, reforming consciousness. "We can't let them find Spark, or Logi."

The choice was stark: run deeper into the Zkazena shadows, or find a way to push back before the net closed completely.

## Chapter 21: Calculated Exposures

The news of Wuxing’s specialized containment teams landed like a physical blow in the strained quiet of the subway hub. Kyber pulled up Zkazena schematics, cross-referencing known Wuxing sensor grids with the reported lockdown area around St. Jude's. The cordon was tight, professional. Their sweep patterns suggested they were methodically searching for specific energy signatures – likely the unique resonance of the feedback blast, maybe Francky's modulated nanites, or even the crystalise itself if they had any inkling of its existence.

"How long 'til their scanners reach this deep?" Kyber asked Cryptolock, tracing potential search vectors.

"Depends on how serious they are and what tech they're using," Cryptolock replied, peering at the map. "These old subway lines have layers of shielding – lead, reinforced concrete, geological strata. Blocks basic scans. But if they deploy deep-penetrating resonance or astral mages… could be hours, could be days. Or they could get lucky." He scowled. "Best bet is usually to go deeper. More rats down here than even Wuxing wants to deal with. Find a null zone, wait it out."

Kyber considered it. Running deeper offered immediate safety. But it meant isolation. Logi, Kyber fiercely believed, needed resources to complete their reformation – stable power, data access, perhaps specialized frequencies Kyber could jury-rig. Hiding in a dead zone might preserve the crystalise but stall Logi's recovery indefinitely. And they still needed to find Francky. Waiting passively felt like accepting a siege they couldn't win.

"No," Kyber decided, shaking their head. "Going completely dark might harm Logi's process. And it leaves Francky free to operate, potentially leading Wuxing right to us if he tracks the boy's implants or residual signatures." They tapped the rumoured ripperdoc location on the map. "This lead near the Tar Creek Undercity access. It's our only active vector on Francky. If he *is* there, hurt, he might be vulnerable. We could get intel, neutralize him, maybe even acquire tech that could help Logi."

Cryptolock looked skeptical. "Walking back towards the heat? Wuxing's probably got eyes all over those Undercity access points now."

"Which is why we go carefully," Kyber countered. "Recon first. Fast, quiet insertion, observe, assess. We don't engage unless the opportunity is perfect or absolutely necessary. If we can confirm Francky's presence and situation, we make a better plan. If it's too hot, we pull back. It's a calculated risk to gain initiative."

Cryptolock was silent for a moment, weighing the odds Zkazena-style. Running felt safer, but Kyber had a point – Francky was a loose end that could strangle them. "Fine," he conceded grudgingly. "Recon only. You smell corpsec, we ghost. Agreed?"

"Agreed."

Preparations were swift. They checked weapons, comms, restocked medkits and basic supplies. Kyber ensured their tactical vest had extra shielding around the pouch holding the Logi-crystalise. The bigger question was Spark.

"We can't take him," Kyber stated. "He's too weak, too vulnerable. And his implants might be traceable."

"And leaving him here alone if someone finds this place?" Cryptolock shot back.

"We don't leave him alone," Kyber said, already moving towards the Citymaster. "We leave him secured *inside* the vehicle, with medical monitoring running, basic life support active. We engage the vehicle's external stealth systems and set internal proximity alarms tied to my comm. The hub itself will be sealed and booby-trapped. It's the safest option for him right now." They looked at the crystalise. "And I'm taking Logi. I won't leave them behind."

Cryptolock didn't like splitting their assets, the worry plain on his face, but he couldn't argue the logic. Moving the unconscious boy through hostile territory was riskier than leaving him heavily secured.

They worked together, setting explosive-foam charges on the main blast doors, rigging pressure sensors and audio pickups near secondary tunnels, camouflaging the Citymaster under layers of debris within the hub. Kyber gently checked Spark one last time – still deeply unconscious – and activated the monitoring systems, linking them to their personal datapad. Finally, Kyber retrieved the crystalise, feeling its steady warmth against their chest. *Stay safe, Logi. We’ll be back.*

With a final check of the perimeter, they slipped out of the secured hub through a narrow service duct Cryptolock knew, sealing it behind them. The echoing silence of the deep hub was replaced by the closer, more menacing sounds of Zkazena's deeper tunnels – dripping water, skittering unseen things, the distant rumble of heavy machinery, or perhaps just the city's tormented dreams. They were exposed again, moving towards the danger, hoping their gamble would pay off before Wuxing's net closed tight.

## Chapter 22: Tar Creek Threshold

The tunnels beneath Zkazena were a different kind of hell than the surface ruins. Less open decay, more claustrophobic rot. Water dripped incessantly, forming rust-colored stalactites and slick patches underfoot. The air hung thick with the smell of mildew, old metal, and something indescribably organic and unpleasant that hinted at the things that might lurk just beyond the reach of Kyber’s helmet lamp.

Cryptolock moved with a quiet confidence Kyber envied, navigating the labyrinthine passages by memory and instinct, pointing out crumbling sections, potential ambush points, and the territorial markers of ghoul packs they needed to avoid. Kyber relied on their multi-spectrum visor, thermal imaging, and motion sensors, mapping the immediate area, constantly assessing threats. The Logi-crystalise, nestled securely in Kyber’s vest, pulsed with its soft, steady rhythm, occasionally flaring slightly brighter. Kyber noted these flares corresponded with passing near junctions known to have high background radiation or proximity to rumoured ley line intersections – *Logi mapping the ambient energy field,* Kyber assumed, finding comfort in the perceived activity.

Twice, they spotted signs of Wuxing’s increased presence even down here – discarded high-energy ration wrappers, far too clean for scavengers, and once, the faint, tell-tale electromagnetic signature of a surveillance drone passing through a parallel tunnel network above them. They froze in the shadows until it passed, the message clear: Wuxing’s net was spreading wider and deeper than expected.

As they neared the coordinates Cryptolock’s contact had provided – an area near the heavily fortified Tar Creek pumping station, a known nexus point for Undercity access – the tunnels became slightly better maintained, suggesting more regular illicit traffic. The air quality even improved marginally, though the underlying scent of damp earth and something vaguely chemical persisted.

Cryptolock signaled a halt near a junction marked with faded industrial warnings. "Should be close now," he whispered, pointing down a narrower side tunnel sealed by a heavy, modern-looking pressure door marred by blaster scoring. "Doc Azrael's 'clinic'. High-end clientele, invitation only. Specializes in weird bio-mods, neural work, stuff the corps won't touch. If Francky needed serious patching up involving non-standard tech, Azrael's one of the few who could handle it."

They backtracked, finding a precarious perch in the crumbling infrastructure overlooking the pressure door and its immediate approaches from about fifty meters away. From here, Kyber deployed miniature optic sensors, sticking them like metallic insects to shadowed surfaces, linking them to their datapad for multi-angle coverage. They settled in to watch.

Time crawled. A few nondescript figures came and went, exchanging coded phrases with a hidden speaker grille near the door before being admitted. Regular clientele, presumably. Kyber ran facial recognition against known databases – low-level syndicate enforcers, shadow couriers, nothing connected to Francky.

Then, a tremor ran through the tunnel floor, not seismic, but the low thrum of an approaching vehicle. A sleek, black ground car, clearly armored and customized, rolled silently into view, its engine unnaturally quiet. It stopped directly before the pressure door. *Expensive,* Kyber noted. *Corporate grade, but running dark.*

Two figures emerged from the vehicle. Not Francky. These were professionals. Tall, identically dressed in tailored, armored bodysuits that screamed high-threat response. Their faces were obscured by reflective visors, but their movements were precise, coordinated, scanning the surroundings with unnerving efficiency. They matched the description Cryptolock’s contact had given for the guards seen with the injured man seeking the ripperdoc.

As the pressure door hissed open to admit the two guards, Kyber’s directional scanner, focused intently on the opening, caught it – a fleeting, distorted energy signature from *inside* the clinic, almost lost in the background noise, but undeniably matching the modulated nanite pattern Francky used, the one Logi had isolated. It vanished as the door sealed shut again.

Kyber looked at Cryptolock, raising an eyebrow. Cryptolock met their gaze, his expression grim but vindicated. He gave a short, sharp nod.

The rumour was solid. Francky, or someone radiating his unique signature, was inside Doc Azrael's fortified black clinic. The reconnaissance phase was over. The question now was how to get through those guards and that reinforced door without bringing Wuxing or Azrael’s undoubtedly lethal security down on their heads.

## Chapter 23: Cracking the Shell

Kyber pulled back from the observation point, melting into the deeper shadows beside Cryptolock. The confirmation of Francky's signature inside Doc Azrael's clinic hung heavy between them.

"He's in there," Kyber stated, voice low and clipped. "Along with at least two professional guards. And whatever security Azrael has."

"So we go loud," Cryptolock growled, flexing his grip on the pipe wrench. "Hit the door hard, fast. Overwhelm them before they know what hit 'em."

"Negative," Kyber countered immediately, shaking their head. "That door looks hardened against direct assault, likely mag-sealed. Azrael's internal security will be top-tier – automated defenses, maybe combat drones, elite guards. Plus those two pros. And a firefight down here *will* bring Wuxing running. Suicide."

"So we wait? Hope he walks out?" Cryptolock argued, frustration evident. "He could have a dozen bolt-holes out of that place. Could be gone by the time we blink."

"Waiting is too passive," Kyber agreed. "Infiltration is the only viable option. Quiet, surgical. Get in, locate Francky, assess his condition and capabilities, neutralize or extract if possible, and get out before Azrael or Wuxing know we were here."

"Quiet?" Cryptolock scoffed. "Through *that* door? With *those* guards? You got some kinda ghost tech I don't know about, suit?"

Kyber didn't answer immediately, instead focusing inward, concentrating on the Logi-crystalise nestled in their vest. *Logi, analysis. Security vulnerabilities. Access points. Power grid.* They focused their intent, projecting the query towards the warm, pulsing object, hoping for some residual insight, some flicker of Logi's analytical genius.

The crystalise pulsed steadily for a moment, then its rhythm shifted subtly. Kyber’s internal sensors, monitoring its energy output, detected minute fluctuations, a faint resonance that seemed to synchronize with the barely perceptible hum of the clinic's power grid filtering through the rock and metal. It wasn't providing schematics or passcodes, but it felt like… attention. Like Logi focusing on the flow of energy *into* the clinic.

Kyber scanned the clinic's exterior again through their optics, cross-referencing with downloaded (and likely outdated) utility maps of the area. The power conduits feeding the clinic likely ran through a specific utility chase nearby. Old infrastructure, possibly less secure than the main entrance. If they could access that chase, maybe they could bypass the main door security or even temporarily disrupt power to specific systems.

"There," Kyber pointed towards a heavily corroded maintenance hatch set into the tunnel wall about thirty meters down from the main clinic entrance, partially obscured by fallen pipes. "Utility access J-17. According to these old schematics, it should connect to the main power conduits feeding this sector, including Azrael's clinic." They looked at Cryptolock. "Can you get that open? Quietly?"

Cryptolock peered at the hatch, then grinned, a flash of teeth in the gloom. "Old municipal locks? Corroded seals? Yeah. Piece of synth-cake."

"Alright. Plan is this: You get us into that utility chase. I'll attempt to interface with the clinic's power systems, create a localized disruption – maybe cycle the main door locks during a power flicker, disable local sensors, or briefly kill lights in a specific section. While their systems are rebooting or focused on the power fault, we make entry. You cover, I lead with sensors. Find Francky, assess, act. Minimize noise, minimize time. Exfil the same way."

It was risky. Tampering with power grids could backfire spectacularly, and Azrael likely had backup generators and countermeasures. But it was better than a frontal assault or waiting indefinitely.

Cryptolock considered it, then nodded. "Better than sitting here growing mold. Let's do it."

They did a quick gear check – Kyber ensuring their hacking toolkit and bypass devices were accessible, Cryptolock checking his wrench and a hefty combat knife sheathed on his thigh. Kyber took a deep breath, placing a hand briefly over the crystalise. *Stay sharp, Logi. Watch our backs.* The crystal pulsed faintly, a silent acknowledgment Kyber desperately wanted to believe in.

With Cryptolock taking point, they moved stealthily towards the corroded maintenance hatch, leaving their relatively safe observation perch behind. The air felt colder here, the silence deeper, pregnant with the possibility of alarms, guards, and the cornered desperation of the Gardener himself. They were cracking the shell; now they had to hope whatever lay inside wouldn't kill them.

## Chapter 24: A Calculated Darkness

Reaching the corroded access hatch was a tense and silent exercise, a dance of careful shadows and hushed whispers. Kyber, leading the way, pressed their ear against the metal, hearing a faint humming – the clinic's power grid running strong, but old infrastructure was often noisy. It wasn't just the sound of power flow; Kyber detected a subtle resonance, the distant rumble of a generator deeper within the building.

They knelt, pulling out the specialized lockpicking tools hidden in their belt. The hatch was old, its hinges rusty and sluggish, but the seal hadn't been replaced recently, the corrosion superficial. Within ten minutes, the lock cracked and fell away, revealing a dank tunnel lined with peeling paint and dusty light fixtures.

Kyber stepped into the shaft, Cryptolock close behind, their footsteps muffled by the worn carpeting. The tunnel was narrow and cramped, with low ceilings. The light fixtures were old, flickering weakly. They paused, listening. Nothing.

Kyber switched on their portable night vision goggles, the world shifting to eerie shades of green and black. The flickering lights revealed cobwebs festooning the walls, dusty vents, and the occasional dead rat. No guard patrols, no security cameras that they could detect with their scanner. They were in.

Now came the tricky part. The utility chase was likely labyrinthine, dimly lit, and potentially rigged with traps. The power grid itself, even in this old section, was a maze of conduits, relays, and junctions. The slightest wrong move could trigger a blackout or worse. But if it worked, they could cut off power to specific sections, potentially blind or isolate sections of the clinic.

They moved forward, the air growing thicker and the air temperature slightly cooler. The sound of the generator was growing louder, confirmation that they were heading in the right direction. Kyber slowed, switching the scanner to its high-frequency mode, searching for subtle electromagnetic anomalies that could indicate security cameras or active defenses.

Nothing. They were still undetected.

Suddenly, a loud creaking echoed from down the tunnel. They froze, exchanging a look. Then, a panel on the ceiling fell loose, scraping its way to the ground. Kyber turned to Cryptolock, who shrugged, mouth set in a thin line. It wasn't a trap, not yet. Just an old building.

As they continued, the generator's hum grew even louder. They rounded a corner, and it came into view – a bulky, old-fashioned diesel generator chugging away in a small room lined with exposed pipes and gauges.

Kyber switched off the scanner. "Bingo," they whispered to Cryptolock. "Now for the fun part."

They crept closer, examining the generator and the junction boxes surrounding it. It looked old, even antiquated. No modern cyber controls, just simple switches and dials. This could be their opportunity.

Cautiously, Kyber pulled out a toolkit, a collection of slim tools and tiny circuits designed for precise hacking. They were about to insert a specialized bypass device into the generator's control panel when they heard a faint click from beyond a nearby corner.

They froze. Something was wrong. Something moving too fast, too quiet. Before either could react, a shadowy figure emerged from the corner, dressed all in black, face obscured by a balaclava, moving with the lithe, predatory grace of a trained hunter. The figure fired, fast as a twitch, a pulse rifle spitting bolts of green energy.

The bolts slammed into the generator with a crackling roar, instantly overloading it and plunging the tunnel into darkness. The shockwave slammed Kyber into the wall, disorienting them, the crystalise momentarily forgotten.

Cryptolock reacted instinctively, swinging his wrench with blinding speed. The figure stumbled, the pulse rifle clattering to the floor. In the resulting chaos, Kyber, temporarily blinded, fired a warning burst from their sidearm in the general direction of the commotion.

Then the light flared again, a sudden explosion from behind that shattered the generator's protective casing, scattering debris and sending a wave of heat rushing down the tunnel. A new figure stepped into view from the blast radius, silhouetted against the bright light. Taller than average, dressed in a military-style uniform with a distinctive emblem on the shoulder, their face hidden by a helmet mask. They leveled a high-powered rifle at Kyber, the red targeting dot flashing in the dim light.

They were boxed in. A new threat. And the situation with Logi and Francky was still unresolved. It was time to act fast, decisively, and brutally.

Kyber's mind raced. A new player, and a powerful one. The window of surprise was closing, along with any hope of a stealthy approach. A frontal assault would be suicide. But inaction meant risking capture, potentially leading to Wuxing swooping in and seizing the crystalise and Spark.

A desperate idea formed in Kyber's mind. A gamble, but a necessary one. "Cryptolock, distract them\!" they whispered urgently, already moving to a specific spot on the generator's control panel. "Divert their attention, make them move\!"

Cryptolock, with a primal snarl, charged into the chaotic darkness. His wrench, wielded like a baseball bat, slammed into the fallen generator's casing, sending shards of metal flying and creating a deafening noise. The newcomer with the rifle spun, startled, focusing on Cryptolock.

Kyber, seizing the chance, activated the bypass device she'd rigged earlier. The generator shuddered, then sputtered into life, emitting a distorted hum as power surged back through the overloaded conduits. The lights flickered, then exploded in a synchronized cascade.

The newcomer was blinded. Cryptolock, taking advantage of the sudden blackout, dropped into a low roll, attacking the blinded figure with a flurry of wrench blows. The fight was brutal, close-quarters, echoing with grunts, thumps, and the crack of breaking bones.

Kyber, eyes adjusted to the returning darkness, focused on the remaining, stationary guard, their visor reflecting the flashes of Cryptolock's hand-to-hand combat. She didn't move, seemingly frozen in shock. Kyber took a deep breath, activated the "Negotiator" persona, and shouted with controlled menace:

"DROP YOUR WEAPON AND TURN AROUND\! NOW\!"

The guard jumped, a surprised yelp escaping their mask. The rifle fell to the ground. Kyber kept the voice low, but it was clear, commanding. "Slowly, and keep your hands where we can see them."

The figure turned, and Kyber caught sight of their face. A young man, early twenties, handsome, and utterly terrified. This wasn't a trained killer, just a hired grunt, clearly taken off-guard by the suddenness and intensity of the attack.

Cryptolock emerged from the dark with a triumphant grin, a bloody but triumphant grin. "Got 'im," he announced, holding the disarmed guard, who was shivering visibly.

Kyber assessed the situation. One guard subdued, the other stunned. The backup generator was still offline, plunging the clinic into temporary darkness. It wasn't total victory, but it was an opening. A chance to retrieve Logi and escape.

"Move quickly," Kyber ordered quietly to Cryptolock. "Keep him quiet."

With a practiced twist, Cryptolock snapped the guard's neck, a swift, efficient kill that silenced the screams before they could reach the generator room. He dragged the body behind a pile of rubble, covering it with a tarp.

Kyber approached the generator, running a quick diagnostic on the bypass. It was holding, the overload had triggered a safety shutdown instead of a total meltdown. A quick reboot would bring it back online, leaving the outside guards in the dark.

Kyber flipped the switch, and the generator roared to life, its roar momentarily deafening. The interior lights flickered on, revealing the chaos – the stunned guard, the overturned console near the generator, the bloody mess where Francky's minion had fallen.

Kyber didn't hesitate. She raced towards the generator control panel, inputting a series of commands from memory. The generator's power output surged, causing a momentary flicker of light, then stabilized. A burst of electromagnetic pulses coursed through the clinic's power grid, a calculated wave of noise and disruption specifically designed to scramble Azrael's internal security systems.

Alarms blared and emergency lights strobed as Kyber and Cryptolock burst from the utility corridor into the main clinic area. The brief power disruption and electromagnetic pulse Kyber had triggered had thrown Azrael's internal systems into chaos, overriding automated defenses and sealing secondary routes, but the primary labs likely had independent power and security.

They navigated the flickering corridors, dodging panicked clinic staff who barely registered them in the confusion. Cryptolock moved with brutal efficiency, disabling anyone who looked like security, while Kyber scanned for Francky's energy signature, which grew stronger as they approached the clinic's core.

The signature led them to a large, reinforced door marked 'Surgical Suite Omega.' Kyber slapped a bypass charge onto the lock mechanism. It blew with a sharp crack, and the door slid open erratically.

Inside was a scene of sterile chaos blended with unsettling organic technology. Advanced medical scanners hung near bubbling vats of bio-gel. Strange crystalline structures grew from nutrient feeders. And in the center, dominating the room, pulsed a large, complex device – the Resonator. It resembled a monstrous metal heart interwoven with glowing crystalline veins, humming with barely contained energy.

Standing before it, silhouetted against its violet light, was Francky. He wasn't gaunt or drawn; the energy feedback at St. Jude's seemed to have invigorated him, though his eyes burned with an unnerving, predatory focus. He turned as they entered, not surprised, but radiating cold fury.

"So," Francky hissed, his voice smoother, more resonant than before. "The meddlers arrive. Did you enjoy dismantling Azrael's pathetic security?" His gaze flickered past Cryptolock, locking onto Kyber, specifically onto the pouch holding the Logi-crystalise. A flicker of avarice, almost reverence, crossed his face. "You have it. The heart\! The very spark I cultivated, now contained... Fascinating."

"It doesn't belong to you, Francky," Kyber stated, their voice amplified slightly by their helmet. "Neither does the boy."

Francky laughed, a sharp, unpleasant sound. "The boy? Spark? He is merely the vessel, the seedbed. Necessary, but replaceable. It is the *potential* he guarded, now condensed within *that*," he gestured sharply towards the crystalise Kyber carried, "that matters\! You stole the key to the New Beginning\!"

"Whatever twisted process you started, we're ending it," Cryptolock growled, hefting his wrench. "Starting with you."

"End it?" Francky sneered. "You think you can stop what's coming? Even now, connections are being forged. Even now, the Gardener prepares new soil." He raised a hand, and complex energy patterns flared around his fingertips. "You shouldn't have interfered. Spark was stabilizing. Now... who knows how his systems will react when his designated connection is miles away, radiating uncontrolled energy?" He smiled cruelly. "You may have doomed him by 'saving' him."

Kyber felt a chill despite their anger. Was he bluffing? Or could he somehow affect Spark remotely? "Tell us how to reverse it, Francky. How to disconnect him safely."

"Never\! That knowledge is mine\! The power is mine\!" Francky lunged, not physically, but psychically. A wave of disorienting mental static slammed into Kyber and Cryptolock. It felt like their own thoughts were tearing apart, filled with discordant noise and flashes of impossible geometry. Cryptolock roared, stumbling back, clutching his head. Kyber staggered, their internal personas fighting for control against the assault.

But Kyber had the crystalise. As the psychic static hit, the Logi-crystalise flared, absorbing the brunt of the attack, its soft light intensifying, projecting a counter-frequency, a wave of ordered data that pushed back against Francky's mental chaos. The attack faltered.

"Impossible\!" Francky shrieked, momentarily stunned by the resistance emanating from the crystalise. "What *is* that thing now?"

Kyber seized the opening. They fired a burst of tranq darts. Francky twisted, dodging one, but the other two struck his shoulder and thigh. Simultaneously, Cryptolock, recovering faster, threw his wrench with deadly accuracy. It slammed into the complex control panel beside the Resonator. Sparks flew, alarms shrieked louder, and the Resonator's violet pulsing became erratic, flickering wildly.

"NO\!" Francky screamed as the tranquilizers hit his system and the machine began to overload. He stumbled backwards, trying to reach the damaged controls, but his legs buckled.

"Kyber, it's going critical\!" Cryptolock yelled, grabbing Kyber's arm. The temperature in the room was rising rapidly, the air crackling with energy. The crystalline veins on the Resonator began to crack.

Kyber didn't hesitate. They grabbed a portable data-spike from their belt and jammed it into the damaged console, initiating a forced data dump, hoping to salvage anything useful about Francky's process or Spark's condition. The download bar filled agonizingly slowly.

"We gotta go, NOW\!" Cryptolock urged, pulling Kyber away as the Resonator began emitting blinding flashes of light.

Kyber yanked the data-spike free just as the download completed. They turned and ran, Cryptolock right behind them, racing back through the chaotic clinic as the building began to shake violently. Behind them, the Resonator detonated, not with a conventional explosion, but an implosion of energy that tore a hole in reality for a split second, swallowing Surgical Suite Omega before collapsing inwards with a deafening roar.

The shockwave hit them as they burst out of the clinic's ruined entrance, throwing them clear of the collapsing structure. They landed hard on the grimy tunnel floor, bruised and battered, but alive. Sirens wailed in the distance, growing closer.

Kyber scrambled up, checking the data-spike and the crystalise – both intact. Cryptolock was already heading for the shadows where they'd hidden their approach route. "Move\! Wuxing or worse will be here any second\!"

They plunged back into the utility tunnels, the adrenaline fading, replaced by exhaustion and the urgent need to get back to the hub, back to Spark, armed now with fragmented data and the chilling knowledge of Francky's capabilities and warped vision. The fight wasn't over; it had just entered a new, more dangerous phase.

## Chapter 25: Fractured Data, Fragile Hope

The retreat through the Zkazena under-tunnels was a desperate scramble fueled by adrenaline and the acrid smell of ozone from the Resonator’s implosion. Kyber pushed their aching body, ignoring the sharp pain in their ribs where they’d hit the tunnel floor. Cryptolock moved beside them, a grim shadow limping slightly but keeping pace, his wrench stained and heavy in his grip. Behind them, distantly, they heard the first echoes of heavy Wuxing vehicles arriving near the Tar Creek access point, their sirens swallowed by the labyrinthine passages. They hadn't been followed directly, but the net was tightening.

Kyber risked a glance at their internal sensors monitoring the Logi-crystalise. Its energy signature was calmer now, the frantic counter-pulsing against Francky’s psychic assault subsided. Yet, it still hummed with a complex internal activity, a low thrum that felt different than before, processing or integrating something new. Kyber hoped it was Logi, recovering, analyzing, but the uncertainty gnawed at them.

They reached the concealed entrance to the subway hub, exhaustion warring with tension. Cryptolock meticulously checked the seals and their makeshift traps – undisturbed. With a shared nod of relief, they disarmed the triggers and slipped inside, sealing the heavy blast door behind them.

The relative silence of the hub was a jarring contrast to the chaos they’d left. Kyber immediately headed for the Citymaster, Cryptolock moving to reinforce the entrance seals. Inside the vehicle, Spark lay on the makeshift cot, exactly as they'd left him. Kyber quickly scanned his vitals via the monitoring system linked to their datapad.

Heart rate steady, slightly stronger than before. Respiration even. Brain activity… different. The chaotic spikes they’d seen earlier were smoothed out, replaced by slower, more rhythmic patterns, though still indicative of deep unconsciousness. Francky's threat about remote influence might have been a bluff, or perhaps the Resonator's destruction had severed a harmful connection, allowing Spark's own systems a chance to stabilize. It wasn't recovery, not yet, but it was a flicker of positive change. Hope, fragile as it was.

"He's stable," Kyber reported as Cryptolock joined them, wiping grime from his face. "Maybe even slightly better. Destroying that machine might have helped."

Cryptolock grunted, peering at the boy. "Good. Kid deserves a break." He leaned against the Citymaster's hull, examining a deep gash on his forearm. "Now what? Francky's still out there. Wuxing's crawling all over."

"Now, we see what we paid for," Kyber said, pulling out the data-spike salvaged from Azrael’s console. They plugged it into their datapad, bypassing the initial layers of security encryption with practiced ease. Azrael’s system was tough, but Francky’s additions were hasty, arrogant.

The datapad screen filled with fragmented files, corrupted logs, and dense blocks of research notes written in a bizarre mix of scientific notation, arcane symbols, and rambling manifestos. It was Francky’s digital mind, partially downloaded in the seconds before implosion.

Kyber started sorting, filtering, searching for keywords: Spark, Resonator, Potential, Gardener, Heart. Cryptolock silently treated his wound, watching Kyber work, the rhythmic tapping on the datapad the only sound besides Spark’s quiet breathing.

Minutes stretched into an hour. Kyber’s eyes scanned complex bio-schematics overlaid with energy field diagrams that defied conventional physics. There were notes on nanite programming, detailing how Francky modulated background swarms to create his effects. References to 'sympathetic resonance' and 'astral anchoring.' And then, the core files related to Spark.

"Found something," Kyber murmured. Cryptolock leaned closer.

On the screen were diagrams of Spark's unique cybernetic implants – not just standard augments, but intricately designed bio-organic circuits interwoven with his nervous system. They acted as… a containment field. A living Faraday cage designed to hold and nurture something incredibly powerful and unstable.

"Project Chimera: Vessel Designation 'Spark'," Kyber read aloud from a fragmented project log. "Subject possesses unique neurological resilience suitable for incubating the… 'Seed of Potential'." Further notes described the 'Seed' – referred to interchangeably as the 'Heart' or the 'Wisp' – as a non-terrestrial energy pattern, possibly sentient, recovered from a 'deep impact anomaly.' Francky theorized it was a nascent entity capable of rewriting reality, and he intended to cultivate and control it using the Resonator and Spark as a focusing lens.

"Holy frag," Cryptolock breathed, staring at the diagrams. "He wasn't just healing people. He was using the kid like a battery… or an incubator for some kinda alien ghost?"

"Looks like it," Kyber confirmed grimly, scrolling through more files. There were logs detailing Francky's attempts to stabilize the Wisp's energy using the Resonator, causing dangerous feedback. The St. Jude's incident wasn't an accident; it was a catastrophic failure during one such attempt, the feedback hitting Logi and creating the crystalise, while also injuring Francky and scattering the Wisp’s primary consciousness – the part now seemingly housed within the crystalise. Spark contained only a residual, core connection. "The 'New Beginnings' resurrections… they were field tests. Using grief and sympathetic links to channel residual energy from the Wisp through the Resonator, creating temporary echoes of the deceased. He was practicing, refining control."

The data was incomplete, corrupted in places, but the picture emerging was terrifying. Francky wasn't just a rogue operator; he was trying to cultivate an alien entity with reality-warping potential, using a child as the incubator.

Kyber looked from the datapad screen to the Logi-crystalise resting near Spark, pulsing gently. Then at Spark himself, still lost in unconsciousness. They had rescued the boy and secured a piece of the puzzle – perhaps the most important piece – but Francky was still out there, and the true nature and origin of the "Wisp" remained dangerously unknown. They had only scratched the surface of the abyss Francky was dabbling in.

## Chapter 26: Starlight Ghosts

Cryptolock watched Kyber hunched over the datapad, the flickering light reflecting off their impassive visor. The air in the Citymaster was thick with the smell of stale synth-leather, antiseptic, and the lingering ozone tang that clung to Kyber like a second skin. He finished binding the gash on his arm, the rough synth-gauze coarse against his skin. His thoughts churned, fueled by rage and a sickening unease.

Francky. The slick salesman peddling impossible hope, revealed as something far worse. Using a kid – *Spark* – like some kind of lab rat, growing an alien… *thing* inside him. Using the echoes of the dead for target practice. Cryptolock had seen plenty of depravity in Zkazena, but this felt different. Colder. More fundamental.

"So," Cryptolock finally said, his voice rough. "Let me get this straight. Francky wasn't just bringing people back wrong. He was using the grief, the memories… to power that machine? And juice up this 'Wisp' thing inside Spark?"

Kyber looked up, their posture radiating fatigue but their focus sharp. "Essentially. The data calls the resurrections 'resonance echoes.' Temporary, unstable constructs fueled by residual psychic energy amplified through the Resonator, patterned by sympathetic links – like Grima's lock of Asa's hair. The primary goal seems to have been stabilizing and controlling the Wisp entity contained within Spark, using the Resonator as an interface."

"And this Wisp… what is it? Where'd it come from?"

"The data's heavily corrupted here," Kyber admitted, zooming in on a particularly fragmented file. "References to a 'deep impact anomaly'… 'non-terrestrial energy pattern'… 'nascent potential.' Francky believed it could rewrite reality. Whether it's alien, extradimensional, magical… the data doesn't specify. Only that it's incredibly powerful, unstable, and possibly sentient."

Cryptolock glanced at Spark, still unconscious, then at the softly glowing crystalise resting near the boy's head on the cot. He swore he could see the light pulsing faintly in time with Spark's breathing. A low warmth seemed to emanate from it, pushing back the chill of the subway tunnel. "And *that* thing," he nodded towards the crystalise, "that's part of it? The main part?"

"The data suggests the feedback loop during the St. Jude incident forced the Wisp's primary consciousness, the 'Heart,' into the crystalline matrix that formed around Logi," Kyber explained. "Spark retains a core connection, like an anchor point. The crystalise seems stable now, possibly even beneficial without the Resonator's interference, but…" Kyber trailed off, the uncertainty clear even through their modulated voice.

"Is it safe? Leaving it that close to him?" Cryptolock frowned.

"Safer than separating them abruptly, I suspect," Kyber replied. "Their connection seems fundamental. For now, it stays."

Cryptolock paced the confined space of the hub platform outside the Citymaster. "Okay. So Francky's plan is bust, his machine's scrap. But he's still out there. And Wuxing is tearing the Undercity apart looking for *something* – probably us, maybe Francky, maybe that crystal. We can't just sit here."

"Agreed," Kyber said, turning back to the datapad. "Our options are limited. We could try to fully decrypt Francky's data, understand Spark's condition better, but that takes time we might not have. We could hunt Francky, but we have no idea where he'd go. Trying to find outside help is risky – too many potential betrayals."

"There's gotta be something in that data," Cryptolock insisted, gesturing at the spike. "A bolthole, a contact, *anything*."

Kyber nodded, already running new search parameters. "Looking for location markers, communication logs, project code names…" The screen flickered, files sorting and resorting. Then, Kyber paused. "Here. Fragmented references under a secondary project file: 'Project Nightingale.' Cross-referencing with partially recovered clinic logs… mentions supply requisitions routed through Zone scav networks. And these coordinates…"

Kyber projected a map onto the Citymaster's side window. It showed the north-eastern sprawl, near the edge of the Containment Zone. The coordinates pinpointed the ruins of the old Starlight Medica hospital.

Cryptolock stared at the map, a grim recognition dawning. "Starlight. Frag. That place is bad news even by Crispyopolis standards. Gutted during the outbreak, right on the Zone border. Rumors say the place is haunted by more than just ghosts – weird energy readings, Zone critters bleeding through, scav gangs fighting over scraps." He looked at Kyber. "If Francky has another lab there… it's deep in the drek."

"It's also isolated," Kyber countered. "Less likely to have heavy Wuxing presence than the central sectors. The fragmented notes mention 'shielded facilities' and 'ambient field resonance.' It fits Francky's pattern of using locations with inherent strangeness."

The choice was bleak. Hunker down and wait, or venture into another high-risk zone based on fragmented data. Cryptolock hated it, but sitting still felt like waiting for the axe to fall. "Alright. Starlight it is. But we go in careful. Place eats people."

"Agreed," Kyber said. "We need to prepare. And decide about Spark."

They both looked towards the Citymaster. Taking him was unthinkable – too dangerous. But leaving him felt like leaving bait. "He's safer here, for now," Kyber paused, meeting Cryptolock's gaze. "We'll approach Starlight cautiously. Reconnaissance is the priority. Confirm signs of Francky, gather immediate intel from the perimeter if possible, and withdraw. We keep exposure minimal."

Cryptolock nodded slowly. "Okay. Let's gear up. Daylight's burning, what's left of it up top. Best to move soon."

They began their preparations, the grim reality of their situation settling heavily upon them. Check weapons, replenish consumables, review the Starlight map data. Cryptolock added extra demolition charges to his kit, while Kyber double-checked their sensor package and hacking tools. Leaving Spark felt wrong, but it was the only tactical choice. They were hunting a monster in a city full of them, armed with fractured data and fragile hope, heading towards the ghostly ruins of Starlight Medica.

## Chapter 27: Zone Bleed

Moving towards Starlight Medica felt like descending deeper into Zkazena’s diseased heart. The relatively stable decay of the central tunnels gave way to structures more aggressively consumed by time and toxicity. Cryptolock guided them through routes choked with rust-colored slime and strange, phosphorescent fungi that pulsed with faint, sickly light. The air grew heavy, metallic, carrying the faint, electric tang Kyber associated with high magic zones, but twisted, corrupted.

Kyber’s sensors registered a steady increase in background radiation and chaotic magical fields. This was Zone bleed – the insidious influence of the nearby Containment Zone seeping into the surrounding reality. They spotted mutated rats skittering in the shadows, their forms disturbingly elongated, extra limbs twitching. Once, Cryptolock pulled Kyber back just as a section of tunnel ahead seemed to shimmer and distort, the air momentarily filled with the scent of burnt ozone and something indescribably alien before settling back to normal.

Through it all, the Logi-crystalise in Kyber’s vest pulsed with a faster, more agitated rhythm. Its internal light seemed brighter, more active, and Kyber’s internal sensors detected it emitting low-level scanning pulses, mapping the chaotic energies around them. *Logi's reacting strongly to the ambient fields,* Kyber noted. *Analyzing the Zone contamination.* It was reassuring, in a way, feeling that perceived presence actively working alongside them, even in this silent, crystalline form.

Finally, they emerged into a vast, collapsed cavern that had once been a major underground transit nexus. Through a gaping hole in the cavern roof, choked with the skeletal remains of buildings above, they got their first clear view of Starlight Medica.

It squatted on a low rise overlooking the nexus – a sprawling complex of interconnected buildings, half-collapsed, draped in creepers that glowed with faint, unhealthy luminescence. Sections of the outer walls seemed warped, melted like wax, likely from the Zone’s initial formation or subsequent energy surges. A faint, shimmering haze – similar to the Zone’s border but weaker – seemed to cling to the eastern wing, the side facing the true Containment Zone boundary just a few kilometers further out. The silence was profound, unnatural. No wind, no distant city hum, just a heavy, expectant stillness that made Kyber’s skin crawl.

"Frag me," Cryptolock breathed, surveying the scene. "Place looks worse than I remember."

They found cover behind a mountain of rubble and twisted girders, offering a good vantage point on the hospital's main entrance and western wings – the coordinates from Francky's data pointed towards a specific research lab annex in that section. Kyber deployed their micro-drones, sending them creeping forward for a closer look, while activating long-range scanners.

The initial readings were a confusing mess. Power signatures, yes – localized, shielded generators running, indicating occupancy. Tech signatures consistent with advanced medical and biological research equipment, heavily modified, clustered in the west wing annex as expected. Communications signals, heavily encrypted, bouncing off makeshift relays. Someone was definitely operating in there.

But layered over that were other readings. Pockets of intense, unstable magical energy. Traces of non-humanoid bio-signs, moving erratically within the lower levels. Strange gravitational fluctuations near the Zone-facing east wing. And the visual feeds from the drones showed sections of the hospital overgrown not just with mutated vines, but with crystalline structures that pulsed with faint light, similar to the Resonator's energy but wilder, uncontrolled.

One drone, creeping near a shattered ground-floor window of the west wing annex, relayed a disturbing image: inside, sophisticated Wuxing diagnostic equipment sat bolted to the floor, humming faintly, but the walls were covered in the same iridescent, fractal mold they'd seen at St. Jude's, glowing more intensely here. And skittering across the floor, attracted by the equipment's energy field, were things that looked like fist-sized insects made of cracked glass and wire, twitching spasmodically.

"Okay," Kyber murmured, pulling back the drone feed. "Francky's definitely been here, or someone using his methods. The tech signature matches his research profile. But the place is compromised. Zone critters, energy fluctuations... it's not just fortified, it's actively hostile territory."

Cryptolock nodded grimly. "Zone bleed's bad here. Those glass spiders? Saw 'em strip a ghoul down to bone in under a minute once. Nasty." He scanned the building again. "Getting *in* for recon looks bad enough. Getting *out* if things go loud..."

Kyber reviewed the sensor feeds, the tactical map overlaying the eerie reality of the ruined hospital. The mission parameters – *Confirm signs of Francky, gather intel, withdraw* – suddenly felt woefully inadequate. This wasn't just a potential enemy lab; it was a contaminated nightmare where corporate secrets, Francky's alien manipulations, and the raw chaos of the Containment Zone collided. How could they even begin reconnaissance in a place like this?

## Chapter 28: Raptor Strike

"Direct entry is suicide," Kyber stated flatly, lowering the optical feed from the drone showcasing the glass spiders and pulsating mold within the annex. "Between Francky's likely defenses, Azrael's original systems, and active Zone contamination... the variables are too high."

"Told you Starlight was bad news," Cryptolock grunted, peering through his own battered electro-binoculars. "Those glass spiders are just the welcoming committee. Gets worse the closer you get to the east wing. Saw a pack of Blink Dogs tear apart a scav crew near there last year."

Kyber frowned, considering options. Purely remote scanning was yielding noise-saturated data, corrupted by the overlapping energy fields. Deploying multiple drones seemed like just feeding them to the local fauna or Francky's defenses. Waiting was untenable with Wuxing closing in.

Focusing inward again, Kyber directed their intent towards the Logi-crystalise, picturing the annex, projecting the need for a specific vulnerability, a weak point in Francky's setup. *Logi, isolate his signature. Find a conduit. Network access. Anything.*

The crystalise pulsed, its internal light swirling. After a moment, Kyber's scanner, still passively monitoring the crystalise, registered a directed burst of focused energy analysis. An overlay appeared on Kyber's AR display – highlighting a specific frequency range inconsistent with the background Zone noise or standard Wuxing tech. Cross-referencing with the fragmented schematics indicated a heavily shielded data/power conduit running along the *exterior* of the west wing annex, likely feeding Francky's specific equipment, using that unique frequency. *Logi found a potential access point,* Kyber thought, hope flickering. *Targeted and shielded – important enough for Francky to protect, maybe.*

"Cryptolock, look here." Kyber shared the overlay. "This conduit. The frequency is anomalous, matches traces from Francky's notes. It runs along the lower western wall, near that collapsed skybridge."

Cryptolock studied the overlay, then scanned the physical ruins. "Yeah, I see the chase. Runs low, near the foundation. Might be accessible from that rubble field where the west entrance caved in. Less Zone critters usually stick to the ground level near structures, prefer the higher nesting spots or deeper decay." He looked at Kyber. "Still risky. Exposed."

"It's our best option for targeted recon," Kyber decided. "Plan: You lead us through the rubble field, find us a close, concealed position near that conduit run. I prep a drone with directional sensors keyed to that frequency. Deploy it for close-proximity scan and data intercept attempt. We get anything useful, we pull back immediately. Anything goes wrong, we ghost."

Cryptolock gave a curt nod. "Okay. Let's dance with the Zone bleed."

They moved out from their initial observation post, Cryptolock taking point, guiding Kyber through the treacherous landscape of twisted metal and crumbling ferrocrete. He moved with an uncanny grace, spotting unstable footing and potential hiding spots for Zone predators that Kyber's tech might have missed. The air felt staticy, charged, and the Logi-crystalise pulsed with a steady, heightened rhythm against Kyber’s chest.

They were halfway across the rubble field, nearing the shadow of the hospital's west wing, when the sound reached them – the unmistakable *thump-thump-thump* of heavy rotors, growing rapidly closer.

"Down\!" Cryptolock hissed, pulling Kyber behind a massive slab of tilted concrete.

Seconds later, a Wuxing Condor VTOL roared overhead, flying low, its powerful searchlights cutting through the perpetual twilight. Markings indicated it belonged to their Special Hazardous Operations division – the containment teams. It wasn't heading for Starlight directly, but seemed to be on a grid search pattern, likely deploying sensors or ground teams further out.

Kyber tracked its energy signature, ready to deploy countermeasures if it detected them. But then, something else entered the picture.

A streak of impossible speed, blurring against the grey sky, erupted from the direction of the Containment Zone. It wasn't mechanical; it moved with a terrifyingly organic agility, like a raptor made of shadow and lightning. It slammed into the Condor's fuselage with explosive force.

For a moment, the VTOL faltered, engines screaming. Then, a secondary explosion ripped through its structure. Fire bloomed against the sky, raining burning debris across the ruins several hundred meters away. The fast-moving shape vanished as quickly as it had appeared, leaving only the echo of tearing metal and the sight of the Wuxing gunship spiraling down in flames.

Kyber and Cryptolock stared, momentarily stunned into silence by the sheer speed and brutality of the takedown.

"...Frag," Cryptolock finally breathed out, his voice hushed. "Things are definitely getting sporty out here."

Kyber swallowed, resetting their tactical assessment. Wuxing was actively deploying, and the Zone's native defenses – or perhaps something drawn by Francky's work – were lethally effective. Stealth wasn't just preferable; it was the only path to survival.

They exchanged a grim look. No words were needed. With even greater caution, checking every shadow, every flicker of movement, they resumed their approach towards the west wing annex and the shielded conduit that represented their only tangible lead, the image of the burning helicopter starkly illuminating the lethality of the Starlight ruins.

## Chapter 29: Leech and ICE

Pressed flat against the cold, grimy concrete behind a shattered plinth, Kyber surveyed the target. The shielded conduit ran low along the base of the annex wall, partially obscured by mutated, razor-edged vines. The air hummed with a discordant symphony of energies – the background thrum of the nearby Zone, the localized buzz of active technology, and something else, a focused, modulated frequency pulsing from the conduit itself. The Logi-crystalise vibrated gently, a resonance Kyber felt deep in their bones, confirming the conduit's significance.

Cryptolock kept watch, his weapon held ready, eyes scanning the shadowed windows above and the rubble-strewn ground around them. The fiery demise of the Wuxing Condor was a fresh, stark reminder of the dangers lurking here. Every flicker of movement, every distant scrape or skittering sound, amplified the tension.

Kyber unclipped the "Leech" drone from their belt. It was small, spider-like, coated in radar-absorbent material, equipped with multi-spectrum sensors and a directional data-tap keyed to the frequency Logi had isolated. "Deploying Leech," Kyber murmured, their voice tight.

With practiced movements, Kyber launched the drone. It skimmed low over the rubble, its micro-repulsors emitting only the faintest whisper. A trio of glass spiders scuttled across its path, attracted by its energy signature, forcing Kyber to make a sharp detour around a patch of glowing mold. Reaching the wall, the Leech crawled upwards, its magnetic clamps finding purchase on the metal conduit sheath.

*Position locked. Initiating scan and data tap,* Kyber thought, focusing their intent. The drone extended a microscopic probe, seeking a seam or vulnerability in the shielding. Simultaneously, its directional sensors began analyzing the frequency pulsing within.

Kyber’s AR display flickered as the initial data stream came through. Thick layers of encryption. Heavy energy signatures consistent with Francky's tech, actively modulating something that pulsed with the unmistakable energy pattern of the Wisp. *He's working with it. Right now.*
